Key findings

What the evidence shows

  1. Existing publishing representation changes the next step.

    PULSE’s official 24hrs client page identifies an established publishing relationship. MLC records for “UCLA” UVAJ2Q, “What You Like” WVARSA and “Lie Detector” LVC6ML show 24HRS MUSIC PUB → Concord claims. Confirm the applicable agreements, territories and catalog periods before proposing new administration. Artist-named publisher claims with empty writer chains are held for entity-control verification and are not counted as apparent gaps.

  2. Separate visible claims from unresolved writer interests.

    Your legal-name variants are evidenced on 171 unique composition groups (186 recordings). Of these, 89 have a positive claim whose own writer chain names your identity; 33 further groups carry an artist-named publisher needing control verification. The remaining 49 have no attributable claim identified in the reviewed data and merit split/representation investigation. Same-title records connected by reviewed recordings are grouped and flagged in the downloadable audit. None of these counts establishes actual payment.

  3. UCLA carries both a valid work and conflicting recording links.

    The sampled “UCLA” recording shows 236,728,076 Spotify plays. Its matching MLC work UVAJ2Q shows 100% total known shares, including 24HRS MUSIC PUB → Concord. The same recording identifier also appears in the returned recording lists for “MY SONG FEAT. 24HRS” MV9SST and “TOP FLOOR FEAT. 24HRS & KELVYN COLT” TD0Z7H. Review the recordings and work identities with the existing administrator before any delinking or dispute; the public data is not proof that royalties were redirected.

  4. Verify writer credits on two highly exposed features.

    “LOWKEY” LVBB2T shows 75% total known shares, and “BACK OUT (FEAT. TY DOLLA $IGN)” BA44BT shows 90%. Their current writer lists do not identify your legal-name variants, despite the reviewed recordings crediting you as an artist. The sampled recordings show 19,060,263 and 12,348,319 Spotify plays respectively. Confirm the exact composition/recording matches and obtain the writing or feature agreements before asserting that any writer credit or ownership is missing.

  5. Recent releases and identity variants need a separate cleanup pass.

    Of 50 reviewed 2026 recordings, 25 have no MLC work identified in the evidence reviewed. Label metadata also alternates Robert Davis, Robert Dozier Davis III and several shorter or misspelled forms. MLC records attach different IPIs to plain “ROBERT DAVIS”; do not merge them on name alone. Verify the primary account, review the recent titles against your administrator’s delivery records, and keep the earlier Royce Rizzy catalog as a separate discovery follow-up.

How to read these statuses
  • No MLC work identified: no matching work found in the scope reviewed; investigate before filing a new registration.
  • Registered · shares remain open: a work exists with less than 100% total known shares; the remainder is not the artist’s personal split.
  • Registered · 100% claimed: the work is fully claimed across listed interests; artist attribution still requires a separate review.
  • Writer attribution needs verification: a matching work exists, but the artist’s legal writer identity is not established on its writer line.
  • Work found · recording link unconfirmed: title and legal writer match an existing work, but the reviewed recording link was not confirmed.
  • Recording-to-work match needs review: an attached work title differs; check audio, versions and agreements before any correction.
  • Needs verification: a possible composition match or other unresolved evidence prevents a reliable classification.

33 further composition groups have an artist-associated publisher awaiting work-specific attribution or entity-control verification; these are excluded from the apparent-gap count.

384 recording identifiers checked against MLC source evidence. Work-level claimed percentages describe all listed interests, even where the artist’s writer identity is not established.

Your next steps

Verify first. Then correct the records.

01 · Identity & accounts

Confirm the writer behind the name

Provide your legal writer name, primary IPI and PRO affiliation, your MLC membership details, and current or former administration agreements. Confirm which writer-name variants belong to you.

02 · Evidence per song

Bring the split documentation

For priority titles, provide executed split sheets, producer agreements or beat licenses naming all counterparties. Written messages can support review; they do not automatically satisfy filing requirements.

03 · Work & recording fixes

Resolve links and claims in order

Confirm identity first, verify the composition and recording match, then reconcile each publisher chain. Attach an existing work where appropriate; investigate duplicates or conflicting links before making changes.

04 · Account verification

Check statements and track follow-up

Confirm whether you have ever received PRO and MLC statements. Track each song from evidence review to readiness, filing and processing. No song is ready to file solely because it appears in this report.

Method & scope

How this review was built

Catalog discovery used Deezer artist releases plus a paginated track search, retaining only recordings whose contributor metadata names the artist profile. ISRCs were deduplicated. Label-supplied composer and author names were captured per recording.

MLC searches used recording-artist names, exact legal writer-name variants and available IPI evidence, followed by recording-identifier checks. Artist searches are relevance-ranked; broad Luh Nae and Yung Wolf searches were limited to the first 200 candidates, and the Bfb Da Packman artist query to the first 2,000 candidates. The complete exact legal-name searches and recording-identifier evidence drive classifications. Missing matches mean “no MLC work identified in this review,” not proof of universal non-registration.

Writer-proof headline counts require a legal writer-name or corroborated IPI match, or a stage-name credit corroborated by the catalog identity and credits. Same-name Yung Wolf writer entries remain unverified where the legal identity is not resolved. Label credits are shown separately. A claim is attributed only when a positive publishing/admin share has a writer chain naming that identity. Empty writer lists and unrelated co-writer claims do not count as attributable. Artist-named or independently client-associated publishers with empty writer lists are set aside for chain/control verification and excluded from the apparent-gap count. Distinct same-title registry records are grouped only when a reviewed recording connects them, then flagged for reconciliation in the composition audit. Identical titles on unconnected recordings are not merged.

Spotify counts were read in the browser for the ten Popular-table recordings per artist. Version-ambiguous counts appear only in the exposure table. Other counts remain unmeasured. BMI/Songview account and repertoire verification were not completed in this review; no conclusion is drawn about PRO registration or actual payment. GMR performer and writer-directory searches returned no work or writer match under 24hrs; a targeted title sweep is recorded in the source ledger.

Catalog scope covers recordings discoverable under 24hrs; this is not an exhaustive standalone sweep of the earlier Royce Rizzy / Rolls Royce Rizzy catalogs. SoundExchange master-side rights are separate and were not assessed. New, removed, SoundCloud-only or uncredited producer placements may sit outside the reviewed set. Data reflects the public sources on 2026-11-02 and can change.

Sources: Artist Instagram · Spotify artist · MLC public database · Deezer artist · Report evidence · Composition attribution audit.
